b1:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["A parent session and every subagent it spawns\ncarry the same ",["$","code",null,{"children":"x-litellm-session-id"}],", so on a pinned lane the whole fan-out resolves\nto one deployment and competes for that box's ",["$","code",null,{"children":"PARALLEL"}]," streams - 5 by default, set in\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"scripts/spark-configure.sh"}]," - so the width that is useful on one box is about 4: that\nbox's ",["$","code",null,{"children":"PARALLEL"}]," minus the parent's own stream, which is why an operator who pins\neverything to one box narrows the queue by setting ",["$","code",null,{"children":"grok_subagent_max_concurrent"}],"\nrather than by switching lanes."]}],"\n"]}]

1f1:["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":["Into the pane: ",["$","code",null,{"children":"Ctrl+Shift+V"}]," or right-click, on a secure context."]}]," Both\narrive bracketed (",["$","code",null,{"children":"ESC[200~ ... 1f3:["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Two scroll-back stores, two different budgets."}]," ",["$","code",null,{"children":"terminal.integrated.scrollback"}],"\nin the settings seed is the xterm.js buffer, which is what a plain ",["$","code",null,{"children":"bash"}],"\nprofile scrolls through; tmux's ",["$","code",null,{"children":"history-limit"}]," is what ",["$","code",null,{"children":"Prefix-["}]," walks\ninside a pane. They read 2000 and 100000 lines here, and the two numbers\ndiffer on purpose because the two stores are billed differently.","\n",["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["What VS Code preallocates from the setting is the buffer's array of line slots -\nxterm.js builds ",["$","code",null,{"children":"new Array(rows + scrollback)"}]," for it up front\n(",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://github.com/xtermjs/xterm.js/blob/master/src/common/CircularList.ts","rel":"noreferrer noopener","target":"_blank","children":"CircularList.ts"}],",\n",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://github.com/xtermjs/xterm.js/blob/master/src/common/buffer/Buffer.ts","rel":"noreferrer noopener","target":"_blank","children":"Buffer.ts"}],")"]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["The cell data lands one ",["$","code",null,{"children":"BufferLine"}]," at a time as output arrives, at 3\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"Uint32"}]," words per column per line\n(",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://github.com/xtermjs/xterm.js/blob/master/src/common/buffer/BufferLine.ts","rel":"noreferrer noopener","target":"_blank","children":"BufferLine.ts"}],"),\nagainst a VS Code default of 1000\n(",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://github.com/microsoft/vscode/blob/main/src/vs/workbench/contrib/terminal/common/terminalConfiguration.ts","rel":"noreferrer noopener","target":"_blank","children":"terminalConfiguration.ts"}],")."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":"The setting costs a pointer array per terminal in the tab either way, and real\nmemory only for the lines a pane actually emits - which is why a busy build log is\nwhere a large value is felt, and an idle prompt is not."}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":"tmux holds its history once per pane, in the server, and every client of that pane\nshares the one buffer. Hence the split: the deep store is tmux's and the browser\nkeeps a short viewport."}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["Inside a tmux pane the client's wheel has nothing to walk - tmux paints in the\nalternate screen, so that buffer holds no pane history - and the 100000 lines live\nbehind ",["$","code",null,{"children":"Prefix-["}],", paid for in the server and not in the tab."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":"Measured on tmux 3.4 with one pane pushed to its limit, 100000 lines of 120-column\noutput holds the tmux server at 381 MB RSS; the same 100000 lines of plain 80-column\noutput costs 60 MB."}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["The same sweep repeated at 120 columns came out close to linear at about 3 KB of\nserver per history line - 10 MB at 2000, 35 MB at 10000, 160 MB at 50000, 308 MB at\n100000 - so this is the one terminal number that turns a chatty pane into real\ncontainer memory, and it is a deliberate trade: the deep store is what lets\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"Prefix-["}]," still reach the start of a build."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["tmux reads ",["$","code",null,{"children":"history-limit"}]," when a pane is created, so an existing pane keeps the\nnumber it was born with."]}],"\n"]}],"\n"]}]
